Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I guess I'm here because... well, I've been having a little trouble at work lately. It's probably not a huge deal, and I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining, but... I'm a hairstylist, and lately, my hands just feel like they’re moving through glue. 

Like, even just doing simple things, like sectioning hair or mixing color, it just takes me forever now. I try to speed up, I really do, but... people at the salon have started noticing that I’m just slower than I used to be. It's really embarrassing, honestly, and I just hate feeling like I'm letting my clients down.

Um... gosh, I really hate to admit this, because I know I should just be stronger, and I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party for myself. I really try to keep a brave face on.

Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I’ve been skipping meals without really meaning to, then realizing at night I only had coffee and a few bites of something, and my clothes have gotten looser.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I’m a burden on everyone and that I deserve what’s happening because I couldn’t keep my money, my job, or even basic salon responsibilities together.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I stare at color formulas or the appointment screen and my brain just blanks, so I second-guess simple things I used to do automatically.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I get thoughts like it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up and nobody had to deal with me anymore.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the sadness got heavy and stayed, the voice at the shampoo stations and the belief that someone was sabotaging my bookings were happening at the same time, not separately.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, I have spent most of this whole decline feeling depressed and weighed down, with only shorter stretches where the sadness was not as prominent.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: over, there was a stretch of several weeks when I was still getting up and functioning more normally, but I was already hearing the voice and feeling sure someone at the salon was interfering with my schedule.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have lost trust from my manager and clients because I make mistakes with basic salon routines, work too slowly, and cancel things I used to reliably handle. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... gosh, I really hate to admit this, because I know I should be moving faster, especially at the salon. I don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything. I really do try my best to keep up.

Um... I know we just talked about this, and I really hope I'm not confusing things or being difficult. I want to be a good patient for you. 

I guess... well, I still try to enjoy the little things, like I said. But maybe I have pulled back a bit from the bigger things. I used to be the one chatting with all the clients, you know? And I'd always try to help the newer stylists when they had questions. On weekends, I'd go hiking with my friends. 

But lately... I don't know, I just don't feel like I have that same spark in me to do all of that. I find myself just keeping my head down at work and... kind of avoiding people. It's not that I don't *want* to be friendly, I just... I don't have the energy for it right now. 

I feel really guilty about it, especially with my friends and coworkers. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person. I'm just trying to be honest with you about how things have changed. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

Um... I know we keep coming back to this, and I'm so sorry if I'm not giving you a clear enough answer. I really want to be helpful. 

I guess... I mean, I really don't want to say that *nothing* feels good, because that sounds so... ungrateful, you know? Like I'm just giving up, and I really promise I'm not. I really don't want to be a downer or a burden to anyone. \n\nDoes that... does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm giving you the right information, I just don't want to sound like I'm complaining too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy levels lately, like feeling more tired than usual or having trouble getting through the day?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I get a little tired after being on my feet all day at the salon, but I think that's pretty normal, right? I definitely don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or anything. I can still get up and get through my day just fine. \n\nIt's really just that... that slowness with my hands that I mentioned before, but my actual energy levels feel okay. I hope that's a good answer... I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really don't want it to seem like I'm giving up or not trying. I know I need to be present and positive, especially at work. But... I guess things have felt a little different lately. \n\nI used to love chatting with my clients, you know? And I’d always try to help out the newer stylists at the salon, just to be supportive. Outside of work, I was always the one organizing hikes with my friends. I really enjoyed all of that. But lately... I don't know, I just kind of keep my head down. I try to avoid people a bit more. It's not that I don't like them! I just... I feel like I don't have that spark in me right now to be the fun, outgoing person they expect. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly. I don't want my friends or my boss to think I don't care. I just don't have the drive for it at the moment. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too negative... I really am trying to get back to my normal self.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I hope this doesn't make me sound like I've just been letting things slide. I really have been trying to work through it on my own, you know? But... I guess that heavy, flat feeling... it's been around for a while. \n\nProbably... um, maybe about a year and two months now? Give or take a little bit. I know that sounds like a long time to just be feeling this way, and I'm really sorry I didn't reach out for help sooner. I just kept thinking I could snap myself out of it if I just tried harder, you know? I really didn't want to bother anyone with it until I absolutely had to. \n\nIs... is that an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements have become slower or that you feel physically slowed down, like you're moving in slow motion?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know I brought this up a little bit earlier, but... I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being bad at my job. I promise I'm still trying my best every single day. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess I have noticed it. It's mostly my hands. When I'm at work... it feels like my hands are moving through glue. Just simple things, like sectioning someone's hair or mixing up the color in the bowl... it just takes me forever now. I try to speed up, I really do, but it's like my body just won't cooperate. \n\nA few of the other girls at the salon have even started noticing... they've made a few comments about how I'm just slower than I used to be. I feel so embarrassed about it. I don't want them to think I'm slacking off or anything. \n\nIs that... is that the kind of thing you mean? I hope I'm explaining it right. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we touched on this a little bit before, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm just complaining... I really am trying to stay positive. \n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess I have lost interest in a lot of things. I used to be so social. I'd chat with all my clients, and I loved helping out the newer stylists at the salon... I just wanted to be supportive, you know? And on my days off, I was always the one getting my friends together to go hiking. I really did love doing all of that. \n\nBut lately... I just kind of keep my head down. I find myself avoiding people more. It's not that I'm upset with anyone or that I don't care! I just... I don't have that spark in me right now. I just don't have the energy to be the fun, outgoing person everyone is used to. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, honestly. I don't want my friends or my boss to think I'm blowing them off. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too negative, I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worse about who you are or feeling like you’re a failure, even when you’re doing your best?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hate to admit this, because I know I should just be stronger, and I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party for myself. I really try to keep a brave face on. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty awful about myself lately. It's just... I keep having these thoughts that I'm just a huge burden on everyone around me. And, honestly, sometimes I feel like I deserve all this... like, feeling this way... because I just haven't been able to keep things together. I couldn't manage my money right, and I'm struggling so much at my job, and even just the basic stuff I'm supposed to do at the salon feels impossible. \n\nI feel so terrible that I can't just be the reliable employee and friend I'm supposed to be. I really don't want to sound like I'm whining, though. I know I just need to try harder to fix it. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I didn't overshare or make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy anything at all anymore, like even things that used to make you happy?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? I mean, I know I just said I'm not doing as much as I used to, and I feel bad about that, but... I wouldn't say I don't enjoy *anything* at all. \n\nI really try to find little moments to appreciate, you know? Like, a nice cup of coffee in the morning, or when a client is really happy with their haircut... that still makes me smile. I'm definitely still trying to focus on the good things, even if it's a bit harder right now. \n\nI'm not completely... numb, I guess? I hope that doesn't contradict what I said earlier. I'm just trying to be as accurate as I can for you. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where even things you used to really enjoy, like a good cup of coffee or a nice conversation, just don’t feel good anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard not to let it get to that point. I don't want to be someone who just... can't appreciate the little things, you know? \n\nEven on the harder days, I still try to make myself enjoy that morning coffee, or at least smile when my regular clients tell me about their kids. I really don't want to seem ungrateful for the good things I do have. \n\nSo, I wouldn't say I've had days where *nothing* feels good. I'm really trying to keep a positive attitude about it all. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you're moving or speaking more slowly than usual, like there's a delay in how you respond to things?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hate to admit this, because I know I should be moving faster, especially at the salon. I don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything. I really do try my best to keep up. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess some people have mentioned it. I'm trying so hard to push through it and speed up. Is... is that what you mean? I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm not trying.\n\nDoctor: Have there been days when nothing seemed enjoyable, not even things you used to really like, such as chatting with friends or a favorite activity?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just talked about this, and I really hope I'm not confusing things or being difficult. I want to be a good patient for you. \n\nI guess... well, I still try to enjoy the little things, like I said. But maybe I have pulled back a bit from the bigger things. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when even the things you used to really enjoy, like chatting with clients or going on hikes with friends, just didn’t feel good or rewarding anymore?\n\nPatient: Um...
